-\theta(t^{\prime}-t)\left<\sum_{\mu,\nu}\sigma_{j}^{\mu,\nu}\{\hat{d}_{\mu,a}^{\dagger}(t),\hat{d}_{\nu,a}(t^{\prime})\}\right>\big|_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t-\delta}&2\left<\sum_{\mu,\nu}\hat{d}_{\mu,a}^{\dagger}(t)\sigma_{j}^{\mu,\nu}\hat{d}_{\nu,a}(t)\right>\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&2m_{a,j}\end{pmatrix}.

(S235)

Here,
\bm{m}_{a}=(m_{a,1},m_{a,2},m_{a,3})=\left<\sum_{\mu,\nu}\hat{d}_{\mu,a}^{\dagger}(t)\bm{\sigma}^{\mu,\nu}\hat{d}_{\nu,a}(t)\right>
is the average magnetization of
a
-site.
The off-diagonal component vanishes because the equal-time response function vanishes.

The Berry phase contribution is given by,

\displaystyle e^{iS_{B}[m]}\equiv\int{\mathcal{D}}[\lambda]\int{\mathcal{D}}[d,\bar{d}]e^{iS_{d}^{0}[d,\bar{d}]+iS_{\gamma}[d,\bar{d}]}e^{iS_{\lambda}[\lambda,m,\hat{m}[d,\bar{d}]]}

\displaystyle=\int{\mathcal{D}}[\lambda]e^{i\int dt\sum_{a}\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}\lambda_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}m_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}}\int{\mathcal{D}}[d,\bar{d}]\exp\bigg[i\int dt\sum_{a}\Psi_{d,a}^{\dagger}(t)\big(\bm{G}^{-1}_{0a}-\bm{\Sigma}_{a}^{\lambda}[\lambda_{a}(t)]\big)\Psi_{d,a}(t)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int{\mathcal{D}}[\lambda]e^{i\int dt\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}\lambda_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}m_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}}\exp\bigg[i\cdot(-i){\sum_{a}\ln\det\Big[(-i)\big(\bm{G}^{-1}_{0a}-\bm{\Sigma}_{a}^{\lambda}[\lambda_{a}(t)]\big)\Big]}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\int{\mathcal{D}}[\lambda]e^{iS_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda,m]},

(S236)

where

\displaystyle\Psi_{d,a}(t)=(d_{a,\uparrow}^{q},d_{a,\downarrow}^{q},d_{a,\uparrow}^{c},d_{a,\downarrow}^{c})^{\mathrm{T}},\qquad\Psi_{d,a}^{\dagger}(t)=(\bar{d}_{a,\uparrow}^{q},\bar{d}_{a,\downarrow}^{q},\bar{d}_{a,\uparrow}^{c},\bar{d}_{a,\downarrow}^{c}).

(S237)

and

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{-1}_{0a}=\begin{pmatrix}-i\gamma_{a}n\hat{1}&(i\partial_{t}+i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}\\
(i\partial_{t}-i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{\Sigma}_{a}^{\lambda}[\lambda_{a}(t)]=\sum_{j=0}^{3}\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{a,j}^{qq}\hat{\sigma}_{j}&\lambda_{a,j}^{qc}\hat{\sigma}_{j}\\
\lambda_{a,j}^{cq}\hat{\sigma}_{j}&\lambda_{a,j}^{cc}\hat{\sigma}_{j}\end{pmatrix}

(S238)

and
\bm{G}_{a}^{-1}=\bm{G}_{0,a}^{-1}-\bm{\Sigma}_{a}
.

We perform a saddle point approximation to the
\lambda
integral, i.e.,

\displaystyle e^{iS_{B}[m]}\approx e^{iS_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda=\lambda_{0},m]}

(S239)

where
\lambda_{0}
is determined from the saddle point condition,

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta S_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda,m]}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}=m_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}(t)-i{\rm Tr}\Bigg[\bm{G}_{a}\frac{\delta\bm{G}_{a}^{-1}}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{\rm sp}[\hat{\sigma}_{j}(\bm{m}_{a}^{l_{1},l_{2}}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})]

\displaystyle-i\int dt_{1}dt_{2}{\rm tr}_{s,\sigma}\Bigg[\begin{pmatrix}G_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t_{2})&G_{a}^{q,c}(t_{1},t_{2})\\
G_{a}^{c,q}(t_{1},t_{2})&G_{a}^{c,c}(t_{1},t_{2})\end{pmatrix}\frac{\delta\begin{pmatrix}(G^{-1})_{a}^{q,q}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})&(G^{-1})_{a}^{q,c}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})\\
(G^{-1})_{a}^{c,q}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})&(G^{-1})_{a}^{c,c}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})\end{pmatrix}}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{l_{1},l_{2}}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}\Bigg]

(S240)

Explicit components are computed as follows.
For
(l_{1},l_{2})=(q,q)
,

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta S_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda,m]}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{q,q}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}={\rm tr}_{\rm sp}[\hat{\sigma}_{j}(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})]

\displaystyle-i\int dt_{1}dt_{2}{\rm tr}_{s,\sigma}\Bigg[\begin{pmatrix}G_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t_{2})&G_{a}^{q,c}(t_{1},t_{2})\\
G_{a}^{c,q}(t_{1},t_{2})&G_{a}^{c,c}(t_{1},t_{2})\end{pmatrix}\frac{\delta\begin{pmatrix}(G^{-1})_{a}^{q,q}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})&(G^{-1})_{a}^{q,c}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})\\
(G^{-1})_{a}^{c,q}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})&(G^{-1})_{a}^{c,c}(t_{2},t_{1}+0^{+})\end{pmatrix}}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{q,q}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{\rm sp}\Bigg[\bigg[(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})\bigg]\hat{\sigma}_{j}\Bigg]

(S241)

As this holds for all
j=0,1,2,3
, this implies,

\displaystyle(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})=0.

(S242)

For
(l_{1},l_{2})=(q,c)
,

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta S_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda,m]}{\delta\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)}\Bigg|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}={\rm tr}_{\rm sp}\Bigg[\bigg[(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,c}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{c,q}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})\bigg]\hat{\sigma}_{j}\Bigg]

(S243)

and so on. Therefore,

\displaystyle(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})=0,

(S244)

\displaystyle(\bm{m}_{a}^{q,c}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{c,q}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})=0,

(S245)

\displaystyle(\bm{m}_{a}^{c,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{q,c}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})=0,

(S246)

\displaystyle(\bm{m}_{a}^{c,c}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})+i\int dt_{1}\hat{G}_{a}^{c,c}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+})=0,

(S247)

or

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{m}_{a}^{c,q}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\\
\bm{m}_{a}^{q,c}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{m}_{a}^{c,c}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}=-i\int dt_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{G}_{a}^{q,q}(t_{1},t;\lambda)&\hat{G}_{a}^{q,c}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\\
\hat{G}_{a}^{c,q}(t_{1},t;\lambda)&\hat{G}_{a}^{c,c}(t_{1},t;\lambda)\end{pmatrix}\delta(t-t_{1}+0^{+}).

(S248)

We will use Eq. (
S235
), i.e.,
\bm{m}_{a}^{c,c}=2\bm{m}_{a}
and

\bm{m}_{a}^{q,q}=\bm{m}_{a}^{c,q}=\bm{m}_{a}^{q,c}=0

from below.
Applying
\hat{G}^{-1}
to both sides from the left, one obtains,

\displaystyle\Bigg[\begin{pmatrix}-i\gamma_{a}n\hat{1}&(i\partial_{t}/2+i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}\\
(i\partial_{t}/2-i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}&0\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qq}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\\
\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{cq}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{cc}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}\Bigg]\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1}{2}(-i)\delta(t_{1}-t)|_{t_{1}\rightarrow t+0^{+}}

(S249)

where the left-hand side can be computed as,

\displaystyle{\rm LHS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&(i\partial_{t}/2+i\gamma_{a}n/2)(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}0&(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{q,c}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})\\
0&(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{c,c}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})\end{pmatrix}

(S250)

Similarly, when applying
\hat{G}^{-1}
from the right, one obtains

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}\Bigg[\begin{pmatrix}-i\gamma_{a}n\hat{1}&(-i\overleftarrow{\partial}_{t}/2+i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}\\
(-i\overleftarrow{\partial}_{t}/2-i\gamma_{a}n/2)\hat{1}&0\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qq}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\\
\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{cq}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}&\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{cc}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}\Bigg]=\frac{1}{2}(-i)\delta(t_{1}-t)|_{t_{1}\rightarrow t+0^{+}}

with

\displaystyle{\rm LHS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
(-i\partial_{t}/2-i\gamma_{a}n/2)(\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})&0\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
(\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{c,q}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})&(\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{c,c}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})\end{pmatrix}.

(S252)

Subtracting the two relations yields,

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}(\partial_{t}+\gamma_{a}n)(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})=(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{q,c}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})

(S253)

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}(\partial_{t}+\gamma_{a}n)(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})=-(\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{c,q}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})

(S254)

Note that Eq. (
S253
) is the complex conjugate of Eq. (
S254
) (because
\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{q,c}(t)(=[\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{c,q}(t)]^{*})
) and is therefore equivalent.
From these two equations, one obtains,

\displaystyle i(\partial_{t}+\gamma_{a}n)(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})=(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{q,c}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})(\bm{m}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})-(\bm{m}_{a}(t)\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}})([\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{q,c}]^{*}\cdot\bm{\hat{\sigma}}).

(S255)

Using

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{3}f_{j}{\rm tr}_{\sigma}[\hat{\sigma}_{j}\hat{\sigma}_{i}]=2f_{i},

(S256)

\displaystyle i\sum_{l,j,k=1}^{3}a_{l}b_{j}\epsilon_{ljk}{\rm tr}_{\sigma}[\hat{\sigma}_{k}\hat{\sigma}_{i}]=2i\sum_{l,j,k}^{3}a_{l}b_{j}\epsilon_{ljk}\delta_{ik}=2i\sum_{l,j,k}^{3}a_{l}b_{j}\epsilon_{lji}=2i(\bm{a}\times\bm{b})_{i},

(S257)

we find

\displaystyle i(\partial_{t}+\gamma_{a}n)\bm{m}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}-[\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}]^{*})+(\lambda_{a,0}^{qc}-[\lambda_{a,0}^{qc}]^{*})\bm{m}_{a}+i(\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}+[\bm{\lambda}_{a}^{qc}]^{*})\times\bm{m}_{a}.

(S258)

Let us now determine
\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(=[\lambda_{a,j}^{c,q}]^{*})
by
finding the saddle point of

S_{\rm eff}[\lambda,m]\equiv S_{B}^{\lambda}[\lambda,m]+S_{M}[m]
as

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta S_{\rm eff}[\lambda,m]}{\delta m_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)}=\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)+\sum_{b}\frac{J_{a,b}(\bm{R}_{a,b})}{2}m_{b,j}(t)-\frac{\alpha_{a}}{2}\frac{dm_{a,j}^{c,c}(t)}{dt}+i\sum_{b}\frac{\Omega_{a,b}(\bm{R}_{a,b})}{2}m_{b,j}(t),

(S259)

which gives,

\displaystyle\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)+[\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)]^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{b}J_{a,b}m_{b,j}(t)+\alpha_{a}\frac{dm_{a,j}(t)}{dt}

(S260)

\displaystyle\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)-[\lambda_{a,j}^{q,c}(t)]^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sum_{b}\Omega_{a,b}m_{b,j}(t).

(S261)

This yields the desired LLG equation modified by the light-induced dissipation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{m}_{a}=-\gamma_{a}n\bm{m}_{a}-\sum_{b(\neq a)}\Omega_{a,b}(\bm{R}_{a,b})\bm{m}_{b}(t)-\Big[\sum_{b}J_{a,b}(\bm{R}_{a,b})\bm{m}_{b}(t)-\alpha_{a}\bm{\dot{m}}_{a}(t)\Big]\times\bm{m}_{a}(t),

(S262)

where we have ignored the dissipative self-interaction
\Omega_{a,a}
.
This completes the derivation of Eq. (6) in the main text.

V
Microscopic derivation of GKSL equation (3)

Our starting point of this work was the GKSL equation (3) in the main text (Eq. (
S83
) in the SI),
which we reproduce below for convenience:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{\rho}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\hat{H}_{\rm A},\hat{\rho}]+\sum_{a,\sigma}\kappa_{a}{\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}\hat{P}_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{a}]\hat{\rho}.

(S263)

Here, the Hamiltonian in the first term
\hat{H}_{\rm A}=\hat{H}_{c0}+\hat{H}_{d0}+\hat{H}_{cd}
(See Eqs. (
S84
), (
S85
) and (
S86
)) is the magnetic metal Hamiltonian described by the Anderson impurity model that we have introduced in Sec.
II
.
The second term describes the decay of localized electrons at site
a
that occurs exclusively when it is in a double-occupied state, which we assumed to be introduced by the time-dependent drive from the laser that couples the double-occupied state to the higher-level state.
We have introduced this term phenomenologically by taking advantage of the fact that Markovian dynamics can always be described in a GKSL form, leaving
\kappa_{a}
as a free parameter that should be determined experimentally.

In this section, to complement our results from a more microscopic point of view, we derive this GKSL equation microscopically by explicitly treating the time-dependent drive from the laser and its coupling to the higher-level state.
(See e.g., Ref.
[
Diehl2008
,
Kraus2008
,
Tomita2017
]
that uses a similar approach to derive the GKSL equation from microscopics.)
We model this system as the sum of three parts:

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm tot}=\hat{H}_{\rm A}+\hat{H}_{\rm drive}(t)+\hat{H}_{f}.

(S264)

Here,
the second term, given by

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm drive}(t)=\sum_{a,\sigma=\uparrow,\downarrow}\Omega_{a}e^{-ih\nu t}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}+{\rm h.c.},

(S265)

describes the time-dependent drive (within the rotating wave approximation, which is justified close to resonance) that
couples the localized electrons to a higher energy level, described by the third term

\hat{H}_{f}=\hat{H}_{f}^{0}+\hat{H}_{fb}+\hat{H}_{b}

given by

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{f}^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\varepsilon_{f,a}\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{a,\sigma}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a},

(S266)

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\sum_{\bm{k}}\varepsilon_{b,\bm{k},a}\hat{b}_{\bm{k},\sigma}^{a\dagger}\hat{b}_{\bm{k},\sigma}^{a},

(S267)

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm bf}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}v_{a}^{bf}\sum_{\sigma}\sum_{\bm{R}_{a}}\hat{b}^{a\dagger}_{\bm{R}_{a},\sigma}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}+{\rm h.c.}

(S268)

Here,
\hat{f}_{a,\sigma}
is the annihilation operator of the higher-energy level state of the site
a
and spin
\sigma=\uparrow,\downarrow
with energy
\varepsilon_{f,a}
.

\Omega_{a}
is the coupling strength between the localized electrons and their higher-energy level induced by the laser that has the frequency
\nu
.
The higher-level state is assumed to be short-lived due to the coupling to the surrounding environment.
We model this by coupling to a free fermion bath
\hat{H}_{\rm b}
, where
\hat{b}_{\bm{k},\sigma}^{a}
is the fermionic annihilation operator that hops between the higher-order state and bath with the hopping amplitude
v_{a}^{bf}
.
Since the higher energy level
\varepsilon_{f,a}
is assumed to be far above the Fermi energy
\varepsilon_{\rm F}
, we assume that the bath fermion is unoccupied at this energy, i.e.
f_{\rm b}(\omega\approx\varepsilon_{f,a})=0
.

It is useful to move the coordinate into a rotating frame to eliminate the time dependence in
\hat{H}_{\rm drive}(t)
.
By replacing
\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}\rightarrow e^{ih\nu t}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a},\hat{b}_{\bm{R}_{a},\sigma}^{a}\rightarrow e^{ih\nu t}\hat{b}_{\bm{R}_{a},\sigma}^{a}
,
the
\hat{H}_{\rm drive}(t),\hat{H}_{f}^{0},\hat{H}_{\rm b}^{0}
are transformed to

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm drive}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,\sigma}\Omega_{a}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}+{\rm h.c.},

(S269)

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{f}^{0}{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}(\varepsilon_{f,a}-h\nu)\hat{f}^{\dagger}_{a,\sigma}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a},

(S270)

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\sum_{\bm{k}}(\varepsilon_{b,\bm{k},a}-h\nu)\hat{b}_{\bm{k},\sigma}^{a\dagger}\hat{b}_{\bm{k},\sigma}^{a},

(S271)

while other parts stay the same.
For later use, we introduce a notion for the drive Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{\rm drive}^{\prime}=\sum_{a,\sigma}[\hat{V}_{f\Leftarrow d}^{\sigma,a}+\hat{V}_{d\Leftarrow f}^{\sigma,a}]
, where

\displaystyle V_{f\Leftarrow d}^{\sigma,a}=\Omega_{a}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a},\qquad V_{d\Leftarrow f}^{\sigma,a}=\Omega_{a}^{*}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}(=(\hat{V}_{f\Leftarrow d}^{\sigma,a})^{\dagger}),

(S272)

Below, we integrate out the higher-energy degrees of freedom as well as the bath that is attached to it, to derive the GKSL equation (
S263
).
We first integrate out the bath degrees of freedom attached to the higher level.
This is performed by using the Keldysh formalism reviewed in Sec.
I.2.3
, which results in the GKSL equation for the density operator
\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}
that consists of conduction electrons and localized electrons
including
the higher level state,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

(S273)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\hat{H}_{\rm A}+\hat{H}_{f}^{0}{}^{\prime}+\hat{H}_{\rm drive}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}]+\sum_{a,\sigma}\Gamma_{f,a}{\mathcal{D}}[\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}]\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot},

where

\Gamma_{f,a}=\pi|v_{a}^{{\rm b}f}|^{2}\rho_{\rm b}

is the decay rate of the higher level state (with
\rho_{{\rm b},a}=\sum_{\bm{k}}\delta(\omega-\varepsilon_{{\rm b},\bm{k},a}+h\nu)\approx{\rm const.}
being the density of states of the bath that is assume to be Markovian.)

We will be concerned with the case where the laser strength is weak enough that
H_{\rm drive}^{\prime}
can be regarded as a perturbation.
Recalling that we are interested in the regime where the c-d mixing can be regarded as a perturbation,
the total Lindbladian can be split into the non-perturbative (
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}
) and perturbative part (
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)}
),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}={\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}+{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}+{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)}

(S274)

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\hat{H}_{c0}+\hat{H}_{d0}+\hat{H}_{f}^{0}{}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}]+\sum_{a,\sigma}\Gamma_{a,f}{\mathcal{D}}[\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}]\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot},

(S275)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\hat{H}_{\rm drive}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}],\qquad{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}=-i[\hat{H}_{cd},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}].

(S276)

Having in mind that the contribution from the c-d mixing
{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)}
would be incorporated perturbatively later in Sec.
III
, we compute here perturbatively the contribution from the drive Lindbladian
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}=\sum_{a}{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}
,
with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}=-i\sum_{\sigma}[\hat{V}_{f\Leftarrow d}^{\sigma,a}+\hat{V}_{d\Leftarrow f}^{\sigma,a},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}].

(S277)

Particularly, we derive below the effective Lindbladian by using the projection method reviewed in Sec.
I.1.2
,

\displaystyle({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{\rm drive})_{n_{l},n_{r}}\approx({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)})_{n_{l},n_{r}}+({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)})_{n_{l},n_{r}}-\sum_{m\in\mathfrak{f}}\frac{{\rm tr}[\hat{l}_{n_{l}}^{{\rm tot}(0)\dagger}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}\hat{r}_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)}]{\rm tr}[\hat{l}_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)\dagger}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}\hat{r}_{n_{r}}^{{\rm tot}(0)}]}{\lambda_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)}}.

(S278)

within the second-order perturbation in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}
.
Here,

\hat{r}_{n}^{{\rm tot}(0)}(\hat{l}_{n}^{{\rm tot}(0)})
is the right (left) eigenstate of the non-perturbative part
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}
with an eigenvalue
\lambda_{n}^{\rm tot(0)}
,
and

{\mathcal{A}}_{n_{l},n_{r}}\equiv{\rm tr}[\hat{l}_{n_{l}}^{{\rm tot}(0)}{\mathcal{A}}\hat{r}_{n_{r}}^{{\rm tot}(0)}]

is the
(n_{r},n_{l})
component of the superoperator
\mathcal{A}
.
The sum of the final term is taken over the fast variables.
(See Sec.
I.1.2
for details.)

The analysis below is more or less parallel to the procedure performed in Sec.
III
.
Similar to the analysis done there, the first step to perform this sort of calculation is to characterize the eigenvalues and eigenstates of the non-perturbative part

{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}=\sum_{a}[{\mathcal{L}}_{d,a}^{(0)}+{\mathcal{L}}_{f,a}^{(0)}]+{\mathcal{L}}_{c}^{(0)},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{d,a}^{(0)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\sum_{\sigma}\varepsilon_{d,a}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}+U_{a}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\uparrow,a}\hat{d}_{\uparrow,a}\hat{d}^{\dagger}_{\downarrow,a}\hat{d}_{\downarrow,a},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}]

(S279)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{f,a}^{(0)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sum_{\sigma}[\varepsilon_{f,a}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}^{\dagger}\hat{f}_{\sigma,a},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}]+\Gamma_{f,a}\sum_{\sigma}{\mathcal{D}}[\hat{f}_{\sigma,a}]\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot},

(S280)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{c}^{(0)}\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sum_{\bm{k},\sigma}[\varepsilon_{\bm{k},\sigma}\hat{c}^{\dagger}_{\bm{k},\sigma}\hat{c}_{\bm{k},\sigma},\hat{\rho}_{\rm tot}].

(S281)

As we will be primarily interested in the case where the drive is tuned to be resonantly coupled to the double-occupied state (i.e.
h\nu=\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a}-\varepsilon_{f,a}
),
we are mainly concerned with the unperturbed state with double-occupied state,

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)];[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]}^{{\rm tot}(0)}=(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}\otimes\lvert F\rangle\langle F\rvert,

(S282)

which has zero eigenvalue
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tot}^{(0)}\hat{r}_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{{\rm tot}(0)}=0
.
Here,
{(\cdots)}_{d}
and
{(\cdots)}_{f}
labels states for the localized electrons and their higher energy state, respectively, and
\lvert F\rangle
is the ground state of the conduction electrons forming a Fermi surface at
\omega=\varepsilon_{\rm F}
.

Again in parallel to what we find in Sec.
III
,
taking the slow modes to be the
T_{1}
modes (where the number of electrons in ket and bra space is the same; see Sec.
III.1.1
)
the first-order correction term is shown to vanish
({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)})_{n_{l},n_{r}}=0
.
We, therefore, focus below on the second-order process,

\displaystyle({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{{\rm drive}(2)})_{n_{l},[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F);(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]}=-\sum_{m\in\mathfrak{f}}\frac{{\rm tr}\big[\hat{l}_{n_{l}}^{{\rm tot}(0)\dagger}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}\hat{r}_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)}\big]{\rm tr}\big[\hat{l}_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)\dagger}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}\hat{r}_{[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)];[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]}^{{\rm tot}(0)}\big]}{\lambda_{m}^{{\rm tot}(0)}}

The intermediate process contains processes such as
(where we did not write explicitly the conduction electron state below, as it is unaffected by
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(0)}
where it stays being in their ground state
\lvert F\rangle\langle F\rvert
),

\displaystyle(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}}(\lvert\uparrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\downarrow\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}}(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert))_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}.

(S284)

In the first operation of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}
, one of the electrons in the double-occupied state (
\downarrow
-electron in the above case) has hopped to the higher-level orbital, which hops back to the original state by the second operation of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}
.
As listed in Table
2
, the intermediate state involved in this second-order process has an eigenvalue
\lambda_{[(\uparrow,\downarrow,F)];[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]}=-i(\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a}-\varepsilon_{f,a}+h\nu)-\Gamma_{f,a}/2
.
By performing a similar analysis to what is done in Sec.
III
, we obtain the effective Lindbladian that applies to the double-occupied state reads,

\displaystyle({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{{\rm drive}(2)})_{n_{l},[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F);(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]}=\kappa_{a}(\nu)\sum_{\sigma}({\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}])_{n_{l},[(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F);(\uparrow\downarrow,\varnothing,F)]},

(S285)

where the decay rate is given by

\displaystyle\kappa_{a}(\nu)={\rm Re}\frac{|\Omega_{a}|^{2}}{(-\lambda_{m})}=\frac{|\Omega_{a}|^{2}(\Gamma_{f,a}/2)}{(\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a}-\varepsilon_{f,a}+h\nu)^{2}+\Gamma_{f,a}^{2}/4}.

(S286)

Here, we have ignored the contribution to the coherent dynamics (i.e., the “Lamb shift”) as it merely shifts the energy level.

We note that the laser drive
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm drive}^{(1)}
may also induce an unwanted decay in the single-occupied state, due to processes such as,

\displaystyle(\lvert\uparrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}}(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\uparrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\uparrow\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm drive},a}^{(1)}}(\lvert\uparrow\rangle\langle\uparrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f},

(S287)

which involves an intermediate state with
\lambda_{[(\uparrow,\varnothing,F);(\varnothing,\uparrow,F)]}^{(0)}=-i(\varepsilon_{d,a}-\varepsilon_{f,a}+h\nu)-\Gamma_{f,a}/2
.
(See Table
2
.)
This results in a decay of a single-occupied state

\displaystyle({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{{\rm drive}(2)})_{n_{l},[(\sigma,\varnothing,F);(\sigma^{\prime},\varnothing,F)]}=\kappa_{a}^{\prime}(\nu)\sum_{\sigma}({\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}])_{n_{l},[(\sigma,\varnothing,F);(\sigma^{\prime},\varnothing,F)]}

(S288)

with a rate

\displaystyle\kappa_{a}^{\prime}(\nu)={\rm Re}\frac{|\Omega_{a}|^{2}}{(-\lambda_{m})}=\frac{|\Omega_{a}|^{2}(\Gamma_{f,a}/2)}{(\varepsilon_{d,a}-\varepsilon_{f,a}+h\nu)^{2}+(\Gamma_{f,a}^{2}/4)}.

(S289)

However, the decay rate of this unwanted single-electron loss process
\kappa_{a}^{\prime}(\nu)
can be made much smaller than those of the double-occupied state
\kappa_{a}
,
by taking the laser frequency to be resonant with the double-occupied state
h\nu=\varepsilon_{f,a}-(\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a})
,
one finds

\displaystyle\kappa_{a}^{\prime}=\frac{|\Omega_{a}|^{2}(\Gamma_{f,a}/2)}{U_{a}^{2}+(\Gamma_{f,a}^{2}/4)}\ll\kappa_{a}=\frac{2|\Omega_{a}|^{2}}{\Gamma_{f,a}}.

(S290)

For instance, taking
\Gamma_{f,a}=10{\rm meV}
and
U_{a}=1{\rm eV}
leads to the ratio
\kappa_{a}^{\prime}/\kappa_{a}\sim\Gamma_{f,a}^{2}/U_{a}^{2}=10^{-4}
.

Combining the two contributions, the effective Lindbladian is derived as,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{\rm drive}\hat{\rho}=-i[\hat{H}_{c0}+\hat{H}_{d0},\hat{\rho}]+\sum_{a,\sigma}\Big[\kappa_{a}(\nu){\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}\hat{P}_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{a}]\hat{\rho}+\kappa_{a}^{\prime}(\nu){\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}\hat{P}_{s}^{a}]\hat{\rho}\Big].

(S291)

Neglecting the decay of the single-occupied state and putting
{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)}
back, we obtain the desired GKSL equation, governed by the effective Lindbladian,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}\hat{\rho}=({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm eff}^{\rm drive}+{\mathcal{L}}_{cd}^{(1)})\hat{\rho}=-i[\hat{H}_{\rm A},\hat{\rho}]+\sum_{a,\sigma}\kappa_{a}{\mathcal{D}}[\hat{d}_{\sigma,a}\hat{P}_{\uparrow\downarrow}^{a}]\hat{\rho}.

(S292)

This completes our microscopic derivation of Eq. (3) in the main text.

Table 2:
Eigenvalues and eigenstates of
{\mathcal{L}}_{d,a}^{(0)}+{\mathcal{L}}_{f,a}^{(0)}
in the fast variable space

Eigenvalue
\lambda_{a,n}^{d(0)}+\lambda_{a,n}^{f(0)}

Right eigenstate
\hat{r}_{a,n}^{d(0)}\otimes\hat{r}_{a,n}^{f(0)}

Left eigenstate
\hat{l}_{a,n}^{d(0)}\otimes\hat{l}_{a,n}^{f(0)}

fast

i(\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a})\par-i(\varepsilon_{f,a}-h\nu)-\frac{\Gamma_{f,a}}{2}

(\lvert\sigma\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}

(\lvert\sigma\rangle\langle\uparrow\downarrow\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}

-i(\varepsilon_{d,a}+U_{a})+i(\varepsilon_{f,a}-h\nu)-\frac{\Gamma_{f,a}}{2}

(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rvert)_{f}

(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rvert)_{f}

-i\varepsilon_{d,a}+i(\varepsilon_{f,a}-h\nu)-\frac{\Gamma_{f,a}}{2}

(\lvert\sigma\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rvert)_{f}

(\lvert\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{f}

i\varepsilon_{d,a}-i(\varepsilon_{f,a}-h\nu)-\frac{\Gamma_{f,a}}{2}

(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}

(\lvert\varnothing\rangle\langle\sigma\rvert)_{d}\otimes(\lvert\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\langle\varnothing\rvert)_{f}

VI
Condition for the emergence of the chiral phase and additional data

In the main text, we have proposed to apply our dissipation engineering protocol to layered ferromagnets, to show that a non-reciprocal phase transition
[
15
]
to a chiral phase emerges (where magnetization exhibits a persistent many-body chase-and-runaway dynamics).
In this section, we provide an estimate for the parameter regime where the chiral phase arises.
We also provide additional data that gives more details on the properties of these phases.

Figure S1:

Phase diagrams at various parameters.

(a) We set
\alpha_{\rm A}=0.1,\alpha_{\rm B}=2\times 10^{-3},g_{\rm B}=-10{\rm meV},n=1,j_{\rm AA}=10{\rm meV},j_{\rm BB}=5{\rm meV},k_{\rm B}T=5{\rm meV}
[The same parameter as the dashed line in Fig. 1(c) in the main text]
(b)
\alpha_{\rm A}=0.02
.

\alpha_{\rm B}=4\times 10^{-4}
.
(d)
j_{\rm BB}=0
.
In panels (b)-(d), other parameters are the same as panel (a).
Here, “aligned” and “anti-aligned” correspond to a static phase where the relative angle between the direction of the magnetization
\Delta\varphi=\varphi_{\rm A}-\varphi_{\rm B}
is
\Delta\varphi=0
and
\Delta\varphi=\pi
, respectively, and “chiral” corresponds to the time-dependent phase where the two magnetization exhibits a persistent many-body chase-and-runaway motion
with
\Delta\varphi\neq 0,\pi
.

As explained in the main text, we study the dynamics of the magnetization of the A (
\bm{m}_{\rm A}
) and B layers (
\bm{m}_{\rm B}
), governed by,

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{m}}_{\rm A}=\alpha_{\rm A}\bigg[-k_{\rm B}T\bm{m}_{\rm A}+\Big[1-\frac{(\bm{h}_{\rm eff}^{\rm A})^{2}}{3(k_{\rm B}T)^{2}}\Big]\bm{h}_{\rm eff}^{\rm A}\bigg],

(S293a)

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{m}}_{\rm B}=\alpha_{\rm B}\bigg[-k_{\rm B}T\bm{m}_{\rm B}+\Big[1-\frac{(\bm{h}_{\rm eff}^{\rm B})^{2}}{3(k_{\rm B}T)^{2}}\Big]\bm{h}_{\rm eff}^{\rm B}\bigg]-\gamma_{\rm B}n\bm{m}_{\rm B}-\Omega_{\rm BA}\bm{m}_{\rm A},

(S293b)

where
\bm{h}_{\rm eff}^{a}=\sum_{b={\rm A,B}}j_{ab}\bm{m}_{b}
is the effective field applied to
\bm{m}_{a}
and
\Omega_{\rm BA}=(\gamma_{\rm B}/|g_{\rm B}|)j_{\rm AB}
is the light-induced torque.
To obtain a rough estimate of where different phases emerge, it is often convenient to linearize the above equation, which gives,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{m}_{\rm A}\\
\bm{m}_{\rm B}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)&\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}\\
\Big(\alpha_{\rm B}-\frac{\gamma_{\rm B}}{|g_{\rm B}|}\Big)j_{\rm AB}&\alpha_{\rm B}(j_{\rm BB}-k_{\rm B}T)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{m}_{\rm A}\\
\bm{m}_{\rm B}\end{pmatrix}=-\hat{A}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{m}_{\rm A}\\
\bm{m}_{\rm B}\end{pmatrix}

(S294)

The eigenvalues of the dynamical matrix
\hat{A}
, given by,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm}=-\frac{1}{2}\big[\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)+\alpha_{\rm B}(j_{\rm BB}-k_{\rm B}T)-\gamma_{\rm B}n\pm\sqrt{\Lambda}\big],

(S295)

with

\displaystyle\Lambda=\big[\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)-\alpha_{\rm B}(j_{\rm BB}-k_{\rm B}T)+\gamma_{\rm B}n\big]^{2}+4\alpha_{\rm A}\Big(\alpha_{\rm B}-\frac{\gamma_{\rm B}}{|g_{\rm B}|}\Big)j_{\rm AB}^{2},

(S296)

determines the stability of the paramagnetic phase.
If the real part of (one of) the eigenvalues were negative
{\rm Re}\Gamma_{\pm}<0
, it means that the paramagnetic phase is unstable, implying the emergence of an ordered phase
\bm{m}_{a}(t\rightarrow\infty)\neq 0
.
If the imaginary part of
\Gamma_{+}
and
\Gamma_{-}
were non-zero, which is the case for
\Lambda<0
, then the dynamics would involve oscillation.
These imply that, when
{\rm Re}\Gamma_{\pm}<0
and
\Lambda<0

are simultaneously satisfied,
we can expect the chiral phase to emerge, as long as the system is sufficiently close to the order-disorder transition point.

Let us now assume that the Gilbert damping rate in the B-layer
\alpha_{\rm B}
is small, or more precisely,
\alpha_{\rm B}\ll\alpha_{\rm A}
and

\alpha_{\rm B}j_{\rm BB}\ll\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AA}
.
This is motivated by the property that it is a necessary condition for the light-generated torque generated to the B-layer ferromagnet,
\gamma_{\rm B}/|g_{\rm B|}j_{\rm AB}
, to exceed in magnitude those arising from the Gilbert damping
\alpha_{\rm B}j_{\rm AB}
, to achieve
\Lambda<0
.
Since

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm B}<\frac{\gamma_{\rm B}}{g_{\rm B}},

(S297)

is required for this to be achieved, a small Gilbert damping of the B layer
\alpha_{\rm B}
is favorable.

With this assumption, the condition for the magnetic order to emerge
{\rm Re}\Gamma_{\pm}<0
reads,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)>\gamma_{\rm B}n

(S298)

and the condition
\Lambda<0
reads

\displaystyle\big[\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)+\gamma_{\rm B}n\big]^{2}<4\alpha_{\rm A}\Big(\frac{\gamma_{\rm B}}{|g_{\rm B}|}-\alpha_{\rm B}\Big)j_{\rm AB}^{2}.

(S299)

Using
(
S298
), we can evaluate Eq. (
S299
) as

\displaystyle\gamma_{\rm B}^{2}n^{2}<\frac{1}{4}\big[\alpha_{\rm A}(j_{\rm AA}-k_{\rm B}T)+\gamma_{\rm B}n\big]^{2}<\alpha_{\rm A}\Big(\frac{\gamma_{\rm B}}{|g_{\rm B}|}-\alpha_{\rm B}\Big)j_{\rm AB}^{2},

(S300)

which yields,

\displaystyle\gamma_{\rm B}^{2}-\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}\gamma_{\rm B}+\alpha_{\rm A}\alpha_{\rm B}\frac{j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}}<0

(S301)

or

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}-\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}^{2}j_{\rm AB}^{4}}{n^{4}|g_{\rm B}|^{2}}-4\alpha_{\rm A}\alpha_{\rm B}\frac{j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}}}\Bigg]<\gamma_{\rm B}<\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}^{2}j_{\rm AB}^{4}}{n^{4}|g_{\rm B}|^{2}}-4\alpha_{\rm A}\alpha_{\rm B}\frac{j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}}}\Bigg].

(S302)

Combining Eq. (
S297
),

\displaystyle{\rm max}\Bigg[\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}-\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}^{2}j_{\rm AB}^{4}}{n^{4}|g_{\rm B}|^{2}}-4\alpha_{\rm A}\alpha_{\rm B}\frac{j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}}}\Bigg],\alpha_{\rm B}|g_{\rm B}|\Bigg]<\gamma_{\rm B}<\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}^{2}j_{\rm AB}^{4}}{n^{4}|g_{\rm B}|^{2}}-4\alpha_{\rm A}\alpha_{\rm B}\frac{j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}}}\Bigg]<\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}.

This yields a rough estimate of a necessary condition for the chiral phase to emerge,
which is

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm B}|g_{\rm B}|<\frac{\alpha_{\rm A}j_{\rm AB}^{2}}{n^{2}|g_{\rm B}|}.

(S304)

or

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\rm B}}{\alpha_{\rm A}}\frac{g_{\rm B}^{2}}{j_{\rm AB}^{2}n^{2}}\ \raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$<$}\kern-8.00003pt\lower 3.01385pt\hbox{$\sim$}\ 1.

(S305)

Figure S2:

Time dependence of magnetization.

(a),(b) Aligned phase, which converges to
\Delta\varphi=\varphi_{\rm A}-\varphi_{\rm B}=0
.

\gamma_{\rm B}=0
.
(c),(d) Anti-aligned phase, which converges to
\Delta\varphi=\varphi_{\rm A}-\varphi_{\rm B}=\pi
.

\gamma_{\rm B}/(\alpha_{\rm B}|g_{\rm B})=1.1
.
(e),(f) Chiral phase, which converges to
\Delta\varphi=\varphi_{\rm A}-\varphi_{\rm B}\neq 0,\pi
.

\gamma_{\rm B}/(\alpha_{\rm B}|g_{\rm B})=1.5

(a),(c),(e) Phase
\varphi_{\rm A},\varphi_{\rm B}
dynamics.
(b),(d),(f) Amplitude
|\bm{m}_{\rm A}|,|\bm{m}_{\rm B}|
dynamics.
We set
j_{\rm AB}=5{\rm meV}
and
\alpha_{\rm A}=0.1,\alpha_{\rm B}=2\times 10^{-3},g_{\rm B}=-10{\rm meV},n=1,j_{\rm AA}=10{\rm meV},j_{\rm BB}=5{\rm meV},k_{\rm B}T=9{\rm meV}
.
The initial state is taken randomly.

The above analysis gives insights into the parameter regime where the chiral phase can be achieved.
Figure
S1
(a) is the phase diagram for the case
\alpha_{\rm A}=0.1,\alpha_{\rm B}=2\times 10^{-3},g_{\rm B}=-10{\rm meV},n=1
(which is reproduced from the main text).
We have also set
j_{\rm BB}=5{\rm meV},T=5{\rm meV}
.
As one sees in the figure, the chiral phase may emerge when
j_{\rm AB}\ \raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$>$}\kern-8.00003pt\lower 3.01385pt\hbox{$\sim$}\ 0.4j_{\rm AA}=4{\rm meV}
.
This is roughly consistent with the condition (
S305
),

\displaystyle j_{\rm AB}\ \raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$>$}\kern-8.00003pt\lower 3.01385pt\hbox{$\sim$}\ \sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\rm B}}{\alpha_{\rm A}}}\frac{g_{\rm B}}{n}=1.4{\rm meV}.

(S306)

Condition (
S305
) implies that the smaller
\alpha_{\rm A}
(
\alpha_{\rm B}
) shrinks (enlarges) the region of the chiral phase. This is confirmed in our numerical results shown in Figs.
S1
(b) and (c).
Furthermore, the above analysis implies that the magnitude of the intra-layer ferromagnetic interaction in the B layer
j_{\rm BB}
is irrelevant.
This is confirmed in Fig.
S1
(d), where the case where the B-layer is replaced with a paramagnetic material
j_{\rm BB}=0
is presented.

We also report here the time dependence of the magnetization in Fig.
S2
.
We note that we did not see any initial state dependence on the phase difference
\Delta\varphi=\varphi_{\rm A}-\varphi_{\rm B}
and the amplitude
|\bm{m}_{\rm A}|,|\bm{m}_{\rm B}|
(except the sign of
\Delta\varphi
in the chiral phase that signals a spontaneous
\mathbbm{Z}_{2}
symmetry breaking
[
15
]
.
